John Gratian assumed the name Gregory, VI. He was revered as a saint for his chastity which, on account of its extreme rarity in Rome, was called an angelic virtue. He bought the papacy with the sincere desire to reform it, and made the monk Hildebrand, the future reformer, his chaplain. He acted on the principle that the end sanctifies the means.

Thus there were for a while three rival popes. Benedict IX. (before his final expulsion) held the Lateran, Gregory VI. Maria Maggiore, Sylvester III. St. Peter’s and the Vatican.(91)

Their feuds reflected the general condition of Italy. The streets of Rome swarmed with hired assassins, the whole country with robbers, the virtue of pilgrims was openly assailed, even churches and the tombs of the apostles were desecrated by bloodshed.

Again the German emperor had to interfere for the restoration of order.

§ 66. Henry III and the Synod of Sutri. Deposition of three rival Popes. a.d. 1046.

Bonizo (or Bonitho, bishop of Sutri, afterwards of Piacenza, and friend of Gregory VII., d. 1089): Liber ad amicum, s. de persecutione Ecclesiae (in Oefelii Scriptores rerum Boicarum II., 794, and better in Jaffe’s Monumenta Gregoriana, 1865). Contains in lib. V. a history, of the popes from Benedict IX. to Gregory VII., with many errors.

Rodulfus Glaber (or Glaber Radulfus, monk of Cluny, about 1046): Historia sui temporis (in Migne, Tom. 142).

Desiderius (Abbot of M. Cassino, afterwards pope Victor III., d. 1080): De Miraculis a S. Benedicto aliisque monachis Cassiniensibus gestis Dialog., in "Bibl. Patr." Lugd. XVIII. 853.

Annales Romani in Pertz, Mon. Germ. VII.

Annales Corbeienses, in Pertz, Mon. Germ. V.; and in Jaffé, Monumenta Corbeiensia, Berlin, 1864.

Ernst Steindorff: Jahrbucher des deutschen Reichs unter Heinrich III. Leipzig, 1874.

Hefele: Conciliengesch. IV. 706 sqq. (2d ed.).

See Lit. in § 64, especially Höfler and Will.

Emperor Henry III., of the house of Franconia, was appealed to by the advocates of reform, and felt deeply the sad state of the church. He was only twenty-two years old, but ripe in intellect, full of energy and zeal, and aimed at a reformation of the church under the control of the empire, as Hildebrand afterwards labored for a reformation of the church under the control of the papacy.

On his way to Rome for the coronation he held (Dec. 20, 1046) a synod at Sutri, a small town about twenty-five miles north of Rome, and a few days afterwards another synod at Rome which completed the work.(92) Gregory VI. presided at first. The claims of the three rival pontiffs were considered. Benedict IX. and Sylvester III. were soon disposed of, the first having twice resigned, the second being a mere intruder. Gregory VI. deserved likewise deposition for the sin of simony in buying the papacy; but as he had convoked the synod by order of the emperor and was otherwise a worthy person, he was allowed to depose himself or to abdicate. He did it in these words: "I, Gregory, bishop, servant of the servants of God, do hereby adjudge myself to be removed from the pontificate of the Holy Roman Church, because of the enormous error which by simoniacal impurity has crept into and vitiated my election." Then he asked the assembled fathers: "Is it your pleasure that so it shall be?" to which they unanimously replied: "Your pleasure is our pleasure; therefore so let it be." As soon as the humble pope had pronounced his own sentence, he descended from the throne, divested himself of his pontifical robes, and implored pardon on his knees for the usurpation of the highest dignity in Christendom. He acted as pope de facto, and pronounced himself no pope de jure. He was used by the synod for deposing his two rivals, and then for deposing himself. In that way the synod saved the principle that the pope was above every human tribunal, and responsible to God alone. This view of the case of Gregory, rests on the reports of Bonitho and Desiderius. According to other reports in the Annales Corbeienses and Peter Damiani, who was present at Sutri, Gregory was deposed directly by the Synod.(93) At all events, the deposition was real and final, and the cause was the sin of simony.

But if simony vitiated an election, there were probably few legitimate popes in the tenth century when everything was venal and corrupt in Rome. Moreover bribery seems a small sin compared with the enormous crimes of several of these Judases. Hildebrand recognized Gregory VI. by adopting his pontifical name in honor of his memory, and yet he made relentless war the sin of simony. He followed the self-deposed pope as upon chaplain across the Alps into exile, and buried him in peace on the banks of the Rhine.

Henry III. adjourned the Synod of Sutri to St. Peter’s in Rome for the election of a new pope (Dec. 23 and 24, 1046). The synod was to elect, but no Roman clergyman could be found free of the pollution of "simony and fornication." Then the king, vested by the synod with the green mantle of the patriciate and the plenary authority of the electors, descended from his throne, and seated Suidger, bishop of Bamberg, a man of spotless character, on the vacant chair of St. Peter amid the loud hosannas of the assembly.(94) The new pope assumed the name of Clement II., and crowned Henry emperor on the festival of Christmas, on which Charlemagne had been crowned. The name was a reminder of the conflict of the first Clement of Rome with Simon Magus. But he outlived his election only nine months, and his body was transferred to his beloved Bamberg. The wretched Benedict IX. again took possession of the Lateran (till July 16, 1048). He died afterwards in Grotto Ferrata, according to one report as a penitent saint, according to another as a hardened sinner whose ghost frightened the living. A third German pontiff, Poppo, bishop of Brixen, called Damasus II., was elected, but died twenty-three days after his consecration (Aug. 10, 1048), of the Roman fever, if not of poison.

The emperor, at the request of the Romans, appointed at Worms in December, 1048, Bruno, bishop of Toul, to the papal chair. He was a man of noble birth, fine appearance, considerable learning, unblemished character, and sincere piety, in full sympathy with the spirit of reform which emanated from Cluny. He accepted the appointment in presence of the Roman deputies, subject to the consent of the clergy and people of Rome.(95) He invited the monk Hildebrand to accompany him in his pilgrimage to Rome. Hildebrand refused at first, because Bruno had not been canonically elected, but by the secular and royal power; but he was persuaded to follow him.

Bruno reached Rome in the month of February, 1049, in the dress of a pilgrim, barefoot, weeping, regardless of the hymns of welcome. His election was unanimously confirmed by the Roman clergy and people, and he was solemnly consecrated Feb. 12, as Leo IX. He found the papal treasury empty, and his own means were soon exhausted. He chose Hildebrand as his subdeacon, financier, and confidential adviser, who hereafter was the soul of the papal reform, till he himself ascended the papal throne in 1073.

We stand here at the close of the deepest degradation and on the threshold of the highest elevation of the papacy. The synod of Sutri and the reign of Leo IX. mark the beginning of a disciplinary reform. Simony or the sale and purchase of ecclesiastical dignities, and Nicolaitism or the carnal sins of the clergy, including marriage, concubinage and unnatural vices, were the crying evils of the church in the eyes of the most serious men, especially the disciples of Cluny and of St. Romuald. A reformation therefore from the hierarchical standpoint of the middle ages was essentially a suppression of these two abuses. And as the corruption had reached its climax in the papal chair, the reformation had to begin at the head before it could reach the members. It was the work chiefly of Hildebrand or Gregory VII., with whom the next period opens.

(1; original 210): This list is compiled from Jaffé (Regesta), Potthast (Bibl. Hist. Medii AEvi, Supplement, 259-267), and other sources. The whole number of popes from the Apostle Peter to Leo XIII. is 263.

The emperors marked with an asterisk were crowned by the pope, the others were simply kings and emperors of Germany.

(2; original 211): Clement V. moved the papal see to Avignon in 1309, and his successors continued to reside there for seventy years, till Gregory XI. After that date arose a forty years’ schism between the Roman popes and the Avignon popes.

(3; original 212): Frederick III. was the last emperor crowned in Rome. All his successors, except Charles VII. and Francis I. were of the House of Hapsburg.

(4; original 213): Die Römischen Paepste des 16und 17ten Jahrhunderts, Th. I., p. 44 (2nd ed.).

(5; original 214): Apocrisiarius (ajpokrisiavrio", or a[ggelo"), responsalis. Du Cange defines it: "Nuntius, Legatus … praesertim qui a pontifice Romano, vel etiam ab archiepiscopis ad comitatum mittebantur, quo res ecclesiarum suarum peragerent, et de iis ad principem referrent." The Roman delegates to Constantinople were usually taken from the deacons. Gregory is the fifth Roman deacon who served in this capacity at Constantinople, according to Du Cange s. v. Apocrisiarius.

(6; original 215): See above § 10.

(7; original 216): Gregory alludes to this fact in a letter to John (Lib. V. 18, in Migne’s ed. of Greg. Opera, vol. III. 740) and to the emperor Mauricius (Lib. V. 20, in Migne III. 747), but says in both that the popes never claimed nor used "hoc temerarium nomen." ... "Certe pro beati Petri apostolorum principis honore, per venerandam Chalcedonensem synodum Romano pontifici oblatum est [nomen istud blasphemiae]. Sed nullus eorum unquam hoc singularitatis nomine uti consensit, dum privatum aliquid daretur uni, honore debito sacerdotes privarentur universi. Quid est ergo quod nos huius vocabuli gloriam et oblatam non quaerimus, et alter sibi hanc arripere at non oblatam praesumit?" Strictly speaking, however, the fact assumed by Gregory is not quite correct. Leo was styled oijkoumeniko;"ajrciepivskopo" only in an accusation against Dioscurus, in the third session of Chalcedon. The papal delegates subscribed: Vicarii apostolici universalis ecclesiae Papae, which was translated by the Greeks: th'"oijkoumenikh'"ejkklhsiva"ejpiskovpou. The popes claimed to be popes (but not bishops) of the universal church. See Hefele, Conciliengesch. II. 526. Boniface III is said to have openly assumed the title universalis episcopis in 606, when he obtained from the emperor Phocas a decree styling the see of Peter "caput omnium ecclesiarum." It appears as self-assumed in the Liber Diurnus, a.d. 682-’5, and is frequent after the seventh century. The canonists, however, make a distinction between "universalis ecclesiae episcopus." and "episcopus universalis" or "oecumenicus," meaning by the latter an immediate jurisdiction in the diocese of other bishops, which was formerly denied to the pope. But according to the Vatican system of 1870, he is the bishop of bishops, over every single bishop, and over all bishops put together, and all bishops are simply his vicars, as he himself is the vicar of Christ. See my Creeds of Christendom, I. 151.

(8; original 217): See the letters in Lib. V. 18-21 (Migne III. 738-751). His predecessor, Pelagius II. (578-590), had already strongly denounced the assumption of the title by John, and at the same time disclaimed it for himself, while yet clearly asserting the universal primacy of the see of Peter. See Migne, Tom. LXXII. 739, and Baronius, ad ann. 587.

(9; original 218): Ep. V. 43: ad Eulogium et Anastasium episcopos; VI. 60; VII. 34, 40.

(10; original 219): Ep. VII. 13: "Ego autem confidenter dico quia quisquis se universalem sacerdotem vocat, vel vocari desiderat, in elatione sua Antichristum praecurrit, quia superbiendo se caeteris praeponit."

(11; original 220): "Servus servorum Dei." See Joa. Diaconus, Vit. Greg. II. 1, and Lib. Diurnus, in Migne, Tom. CV. 23. Augustin (Epist. 217, ad Vitalem) had before subscribed himself: "Servus Christi, et per ipsum servus servorum ejus." Comp. Matt. xx. 26; xxiii. II. Fulgentius styled himself "Servorum Christi famulus." The popes ostentatiously wash the beggars’ feet at St. Peter’s in holy week, in imitation of Christ’s example, but expect kings and queens to kiss their toe.

(12; original 221): His letter "ad Phocam imperatorem," Ep. XIII. 31 (III. 1281 in Migne) begins with "Gloria in excelsis Deo, qui juxta quod scriptum est, immutat tempora et transfert regna." Comp. his letter "ad Leontiam imperatricen" (Ep. XIII. 39).

(13; original 222): Gibbon (ch. 46): "As a subject and a Christian, it was the duty of Gregory to acquiesce in the established government; but the joyful applause with which he salutes the fortune of the assassin, has sullied, with indelible disgrace, the character of the saint." Milman (II. 83): "The darkest stain on the name of Gregory is his cruel and unchristian triumph in the fall of the Emperor Maurice-his base and adulatory praise of Phocas, the most odious and Sanguinary tyrant who had ever seized the throne of Constantinople." Montalembert says (II. 116): "This is the only stain in the life of Gregory. We do not attempt either to conceal or excuse it .... Among the greatest and holiest of mortals, virtue, like wisdom, always falls short in some respect." It is charitable to assume, with Baronius and other Roman Catholic historians, that Gregory, although usually very well informed, at the time he expressed his extravagant joy at the elevation of Phocas, knew only the fact, and not the bloody means of the elevation. The same ignorance must be assumed in the case of his flattering letters to Brunhilde, the profligate and vicious fury of France. Otherwise we would have here on a small scale an anticipation of the malignant joy with which Gregory XIII. hailed the fearful slaughter of the Huguenots.

(14; original 223): The words run thus: "Hic [Phocas] rogante papa Bonifacio statuit Romanae et apostolicae ecclesia caput esse omniuim ecclesiarum, quia ecclesia Constantinopolitana primam se omnium rum scribebat." Paulus Diaconus, De Gest. Lomb. IV., cap. 7, in Muratori, Rer. Ital., I. 465. But the authenticity of this report which was afterwards frequently copied, is doubtful. It has been abused by controversialists on both sides. It is not the first declaration of the Roman primacy, nor is it a declaration of an exclusive primacy, nor an abrogation of the title of "oecumenical patriarch" on the part of the bishop of Constantinople. Comp. Greenwood, vol. II. 239 sqq.

(15; original 224): Ep. VII. 40 (Migne III. 899). This parallel between the three great sees of Peter—a hierarchical tri-personality in unity of essence—seems to be entirely original with Gregory, and was never used afterwards by a Roman pontiff. It is fatal to the sole primacy of the Roman chair of Peter, and this is the very essence of popery.

(16; original 225): Ep. VIII. 30 (III. 933).

(17; original 226): Epist. V. 20 (III. 745). He quotes in proof the pet-texts of popery, John xxi. 17; Luke xxii. 31; Matt. xvi. 18.

(18; original 227): Such titles as Universalis Episcopus (used by Boniface III., a year after Gregory’s death), Pontifex Maximus, Summus Pontifex, Virarius Christi, and even "ipsius Dei in terris Virarius" (Conc. Trid. VI. De reform., c. 1). First Vicar of Peter, then Vicar of Christ, at last Vicar of God Almighty!

(19; original 228): Ep. missoria, cap. 3 (ed. Migne I. 513): "Primum quidem fundamenta historice ponimus; deinde per significatinem typicam in artem fidei fabricam mentis erigimus; ad extremum per moralitatus gratiam, quasi superducto aedificium colore vestimus."

(20; original 229): See "Hymns Ancient and Modem."

(21; original 230): · Comp. Barmby, Greg. the Gr., pp. 188-190; Lau, p. 262; Ebert I. 519.

(22; original 231): Or Luitprand, born about 690, died 744. There is also a Lombard historian of that name, a deacon of the cathedral of Pavia, afterwards bishop of Cremona, died 972.

(23; original 232): Gibbon actually attributes these titles to Charles Martel; while Bryce (p. 40) thinks that they were first given to Pepin. Gregory II. had already (724) addressed Charles Martel as "Patricius" (see Migne, Opera Caroli M. II. 69). Gregory III. sent him in 739 ipsas sacratissimas claves confessionis beati Petri quas vobus ad regnum dimisimus (ib. p. 66), which implies the transfer of civil authority over Rome.

(24; original 233): Milman (Book IV., ch. 9) says that Dante, the faithful recorder of popular Catholic tradition, adopts the condemnatory legend which puts Charles "in the lowest pit of hell." But I can find no mention of him in Dante. The Charles Martel of Parad. VIII. and IX. is a very, different person, a king of Hungary, who died 1301. See Witte’s Dante, p. 667, and Carey’s note on Par. VIII. 53. On the relations of Charles Martel to Boniface see Rettberg, Kirchengesch. Deutschlands, I. 306 sqq.

(25; original 234): Or Pipin, Pippin, Pippinus. The last is the spelling in his documents.

(26; original 235): Rettberg, however (I. 385 sqq.), disconnects Boniface from all participation in the elevation and coronation of Pepin, and represents him as being rather opposed to it. He argues from the silence of some annalists, and from the improbability that the pope should have repeated the consecration if it had been previously performed by his legate.

(27; original 236): This is the enumeration of Baronius ad ann. 755. Others define the extent differently. Comp. Wiltsch, Kirchl. Geographie und Statistik, I. pp. 246 sqq.

(28; original 237): Constantine bestowed upon the pope a portion of the Lateran palace for his residence, and upon the church the right to hold real estate and to receive bequests of landed property from individuals. This is the slender foundation for the fable of the Donatio Constantini.

(29; original 238): Inferno xix. 115-118:

"Ahi Costantin, di quanto mal fu matre,

Non la tua conversion, ma quella dote,

Che da te presse il primo ricco patre!"

(30; original 239): Paradiso XX. 57-60; VI. 94-97. Longfellow’s translation.

(31; original 240): Joseph de Maistre: "Cet homme est si grand que, la grandeur a pénétré son nom." (ch. 4),

(32; original 241): "It would be folly," says Eginhard "to write a word about the birth and infancy or even the boyhood of Charles, for nothing has ever been written on the subject, and there is no one alive who can give information about it." His birth is usually assigned to April 2, 742, at Aix-la-Chapelle; but the legend makes him the child of illegitimate love, who grew up wild as a miller’s son in Bavaria. His name is mentioned only twice before be assumed the reins of government, once at a court reception given by his father to pope Stephen II., and once as a witness in the Aquitanian campaigns.

(33; original 242): According to the enumeration of Eginhard (ch. 33), who, however, gives only 21, omitting Narbonne. Charles bequeathed one-third of his treasure and moveable goods to the metropolitan sees.

(34; original 243): The magnificent portrait of Charles by Albrecht Dürer is a fancy picture, and not sustained by the oldest representations. Vétault gives several portraits, and discusses them, p. 540.

(35; original 244): Wintermonat for January, Hornung for February, Lenz for March, Ostermonat for April, etc. See Eginhard, ch. 29.

(36; original 245): Eginhard, ch. 27.

(37; original 246): Bossuet justified all his conquests because they were an extension of Christianity."Les conquêtes prodigieuses," he says, "furent la dilatation du règne de Dieu, et il se moutra très chrétien dans toutes ses aeuvres."

(38; original 247): Pope Stephen III. protested, indeed, in the most violent language against the second marriage of Charles with Desiderata, a daughter of the king of Lombardy, but not on the ground of divorce from his first wife, which would have furnished a very good reason, but from opposition to a union with the "perfidious, leprous, and fetid brood of the Lombards, a brood hardly reckoned human." Charles married the princess, to the delight of his mother, but repudiated her the next year and sent her back to her father. See Milman, Bk. IV., ch. 12 (II. 439).

(39; original 248): "Maximo totius populi luctu, " says Eginhard.

(40; original 249): The historic foundation of this defeat is given by Eginhard, ch. 9. It was then marvellously embellished, and Roland became the favorite theme of minstrels and poets, as Théroulde’s Chanson de Roland, Turpin’s Chroniqué, Bojardo’s Orlando Innamorato, Ariosto’s Orlando Furioso, etc. His enchanted Horn sounded so loud that the birds fell dead at its blast, and the whole Saracen army drew back terror-struck. When he was attacked in the Pyrenees, he blew the horn for the last time so hard that the veins of his neck started, and Charlemagne heard it several miles off at St. Jean Pied de Port, but too late to save

"The dead who, deathless all,

Were slain at famous Roncevall."

(41; original 250): Annales Laurissenses ad ann. 801: "Ipsa die sacratissima natalis Domini cum Rex ad Missam ante confessionem b. Petri Apostoli ab oratione surgeret, Leo P. coronam capriti ejus imposuit, et a cuncto Romanorum populo acclamatum est:, Karolo Augusto, a Deo coronato, magno et pacifico Imperatori Romanorum, vita et victoria!’ Et post Laudes ab Apostolico more antiquorum principum adoratus est, atque, ablato Patricii nomine, Imperator et Augustus est appellatus." Comp. Eginhard, Annal. ad ann. 800, and Vita Car., c. 28.

(42; original 251): But the date of the letter and the meaning of imperialis are not quite certain. See Rettberg, Kirchengesch. Deutschlands, I. 430, and Baxmann, Politik der Päpste, I. 313 sqq.

(43; original 252): The picture is reproduced in the works of Vétault and Stacke above quoted.

(44; original 253): Milman (II. 497): "The Council of Frankfort displays most fully the power assumed by Charlemagne over the hierarchy as well as the nobility of the realm, the mingled character, the all-embracing comprehensiveness of his legislation. The assembly at Frankfort was at once a Diet or Parliament of the realm and an ecclesiastical Council. It took cognizance alternately of matters purely ecclesiastical and of matters as clearly, secular. Charlemagne was present and presided in the Council of Frankfort. The canons as well as the other statutes were issued chiefly in his name."

(45; original 254): Sanctae Ecclesiae tam pium ac devotum in servitio Dei rectorem. Also, in his own language, Devotus Ecclesiae defensor atque adjutor in omnibus apostolicae sedis. Rettberg I. 425, 439 sqq.

(46; original 255): Ann. Einhardi, ad. ann. 813 (in Migne’s Patrol. Tom. 104, p. 478): Evocatum ad se apud Aquasgrani filium suum Illudovicum Aquitaniae regem, coronam illi imposuit et imperialis nominis sibi consortem fecit.’ When Stephen IV. visited Louis in 816, he bestowed on him simply spiritual consecration. In the same manner Louis appointed his son Lothair emperor who was afterwards crowned by the pope in Rome (823).

(47; original 256): Bryce, p. 396 (8th ed.)

(48; original 257): Friedrich Rückert has reproduced this significant German legend in a poem beginning:

Der alte Barbarossa,

Der Kaiser Friederich,

Im unterird’schen Schlosse

Hält er verzaubert sich.

Er ist niemals gestorben,

Er lebt darin noch jetzt;

Er hat im Schloss verborgen

Zum Schlaf sich hingesetzt.

Er hat hinabgenommen

Des Reiches Herrlichkeit,

Und wird einst wiederkommen

Mit ihr zu seiner Zeit, "etc.

(49; original 258): He alone, of all the emperors, is consigned to hell by Dante (Inferno, x. 119):

"Within here is the second Frederick."

(50; original 259): Schiller calls it "die kaiserlose, die schreckliche Zeit."

(51; original 260): The pope Pius VI. even made a journey to Vienna, but when he extended his hand to the minister Kaunitz to kiss, the minister took it and shook it. Joseph in turn visited Rome, and was received by the people with the shout: "Evviva il nostro imperatore!"

(52; original 261): Dante (Purgat. VII. 94) represents Rudolf of Hapsburg as seated gloomily apart in purgatory, and mourning his sin of neglecting

"To heal the wounds that Italy have slain."

Weary of the endless strife of domestic tyrants and factions in every city, Dante longed for some controlling power that should restore unity and peace to his beloved but unfortunate Italy. He expounded his political ideas in his work De Monarchia.

(53; original 262): In another letter to Fesch (Correspond. de l’ empereur Napol. Ier, Tom. xi. 528), he writes, "Pour le pape je suis Charemagne. parce que comme Charlemagne je réunis la couronne de Prance à celle du Lombards et que mon empire confine avec l’ Orient." Quoted by Bryce.

(54; original 263): The oldest testimony in the almost contemporary "Liber Pontificalis" of Anastasius is wanting in the best manuscripts, and must be a later interpolation. Döllinger shows that the myth, although it may have circulated earlier in the mouth of the people, was not definitely put into writing before the middle of the thirteenth century.

(55; original 264): The preface begins: "Isidorus Mercator servus Christi lectori conservo suo et parenti suo in Domino fideli (al. fidei) salutem.’ The byname "Mercator," which is found in 30 of the oldest codices, is so far unexplained. Some refer it to Marius Mercator, a learned Occidental layman residing in Constantinople, who wrote against Pelagius and translated ecclesiastical records which pseudo-Isidorus made use of. Others regard it as a mistake for " Peccator" (a title of humility frequently used by priests and bishops, e.g. by St. Patrick in his " Confession"), which is found in 3 copies. " Mercatus" also occurs it, several copies, and this would be equivalent to redemptus, " Isidorus, the redeemed servant of Christ." See Hinschius and Richter, l.c.

(56; original 265): The original name was decretale constitutum or decretalis epistola, afterwards decretalis. See Richter, l.c. p. 80.

(57; original 266): The forgery was first suggested by Nicolaus de Cusa, in the fifteenth century, and Calvin (Inst. IV. 7, 11, 20), and then proved by the Magdeburg Centuries, and more conclusively by the Calvinistic divine David Blondel (1628) against the attempted vindication of the Jesuit Torres (Turrianus, 1572). The brothers Ballerini, Baronius, Bellarmin, Theiner, Walter, Möhler, Hefele, and other Roman Catholic scholars admit the forgery, but usually try to mitigate it and to underrate the originality and influence of Pseudo-Isidor. Some Protestant divines have erred in the opposite direction (as Richter justly observes, l.c. p. 117).

(58; original 267): "Dominis meis beatissimis Petro et Paulo, et per eos etiam beato Sylvestro Patri nostro summo pontifici, et universalis urbis Romae papae, et omnibus ejus successoribus pontificibus . . concedimus palatium imperii nostri Lateranense ... deinde diadema, videlicet coronam capitis nostri simulque pallium, vel mitram .... . et omnia imperialia indumenta ... et imperialia sceptra . . et omnem possessionem imperialis culminis et gloriam potestatis nostrae ... Unde ut pontificalis apex non vilescat, sed magis amplius quam terreni imperii dignitas et gloriae potentia decoretur, ecce tam palatium nostrum, ut praedictum est, quamque Pomanae vobis et omnes Italiae seu occidentalium regionum provincias, loca et civitates beatissimo pontifici nostro, Sylvestro universali papae, concedimus atque relinquimus." In Migne, Tom. 130, p. 249 sq.

(59; original 268): That Constantine made donations to Sylvester on occasion of his pretended baptism is related first in the Acta Sylvestri, then by Hadrian I. in a letter to Charlemagne (780). In the ninth century the spurious document appeared. The spuriousness was perceived as early as 999 by the emperor Otho III. and proven by Laurentius Valla about 1440 in De falso credita et ementita Constantini donatione. The document is universally given up as a fiction, though Baronius defended the donation itself.

(60; original 269): The following persons have been suggested as authors: Benedictus Levita (Deacon) of Mayence, whose Capitularium of about 847 agrees in several passages literally with the Decretals (Blondel, Knust, Walter); Rothad of Soissons (Phillips, Gfrörer); Otgar, archbishop of Mayence, who took a prominent part in the clerical rebellion against Louis the Pious (Ballerinii, Wasserschleben); Ebo, archbishop of Rheims, the predecessor of Hincmar and leader in that rebellion, or some unknown ecclesiastic in that diocese (Weizsäcker, von Noorden, Hinschius, Richter, Baxmann). The repetitions suggest a number of authors and a gradual growth.

(61; original 270): Nicolai I. Epist. ad universos episcopos Galliae ann. 865 (Mansi xv. p. 694 sq.): "Decretales epistolae Rom. Pontificum sunt recipiendae, etiamsi non sunt canonum codici compaginatae: quoniam inter ipsos canones unum b. Leonis capitulum constat esse permixtum, quo omnia decretalia constituta sedes apostolicae custodiri mandantur.—Itaque nihil interest, utrum sint omnia decretalia sedis Apost. constituta inter canones conciliorum immixta, cum omnia in uno copore compaginare non possint et illa eis intersint, quae firmitatem his quae desunt et vigorem suum assignet.—Sanctus Gelasius (quoque) non dixit suscipiendas decretales epistolas quae inter canones habentur, nec tantum quas moderni pontifices ediderunt, sed quas beatissimi Papae diversis temporibus ab urbe Roma dederunt."

(62; original 271): Jaffé, 246 and 247, and Mansi, XV. 687 sqq.

(63; original 272): Rotha dum canonice ... dejectum et a Nicolao papa non regulariter, sed potentialiter restitutum." See Baxmann, II. 26.

(64; original 273): According to Auxentius and Vulgarius, pope Stephen VII. was the author of the outrage on the corpse of Formosus; Liutprand traces it to Sergius III. in 898, when he was anti-pope of John IX. Baronius conjectures that Liutprand wrote Sergius for Stephanus. Hefele assents, Conciliengesch. IV. 561 sqq.

(65; original 274): Höfler (I. 16) asserts that every princely family of Italy in the tenth century was tainted with incestuous blood, and that it was difficult to distinguish wives and sisters mothers and daughters. See his genealogical tables appended to the first volume.

(66; original 275): Liutprandi Antapodosis, II. 48 (Pertz, V. 297; Migne, CXXXVI. 827): Theodora, scortum impudens ... (quod dictu etiam foedissimum est), Romanae civitatis non inviriliter monarchiam obtinebat. Quae duas habuit natas, Marotiam atque Theodoram, sibi non solum coaequales, verum etiam Veneris exercitio promptiores. Harum Marotia ex Papa Sergio-Joannem, qui post Joannis Ravennatis obitum Romanae Ecclesiae obtinuit dignitatem, nefario genuit adulterio, "etc. In the same ch. he calls the elder Theodora "meretrix satis impudentissima, Veneris calore succensa."

This Theodora was the wife of Theophylactus, Roman Consul and Senator, probably of Byzantine origin, who appears in 901 among the Roman judges of Louis III. She called herself " Senatrix." She was the mistress of Adalbert of Tuscany, called the Rich (d. 926), and of pope John X. (d. 928). And yet she is addressed by Eugenius Vulgarius as "sanctissima et venerabilis matrona!" (See Dümmler, l.c. p. 146, and Hefele, IV. 575.) Her daughter Marozia (or Maruccia, the diminutive of Maria, Mariechen) was the boldest and most successful of the three. She was the mistress of pope Sergius III. and of Alberic I., Count of Tusculum (d. 926), and married several times. Comp. Liutprand, III. 43 and 44. She perpetuated her rule through her son, Alberic II., and her grandson, pope John XII. With all their talents and influence, these strong-minded women were very, ignorant; the daughters of the younger Theodora could neither read nor write, and signed their name in 945 with a +. (Gregorovius, III. 282 sq.) The Tusculan popes and the Crescentii, who controlled and disgraced the papacy in the eleventh century, were descendants of the same stock.

The main facts of this shameful reign rest on good contemporary Catholic authorities (as Liutprand, Flodoard, Ratherius of Verona, Benedict of Soracte, Gerbert, the transactions of the Councils in Rome, Rheims, etc.), and are frankly admitted with devout indignation by Baronius and other Roman Catholic historians, but turned by them into an argument for the divine origin of the papacy, whose restoration to power appears all the more wonderful from the depth of its degradation. Möhler (Kirchgesch. ed. by Gama, II. 183) calls Sergius III., John X., John XI., and John XII." horrible popes," and says that " crimes alone secured the papal dignity!" Others acquit the papacy of guilt, since it was not independent. The best lesson which Romanists might derive from this period of prostitution is humility and charity. It is a terrible rebuke to pretensions of superior sanctity.

(67; original 276): Baronius, following Liutprand, calls Sergius "homo vitiorum omnium servus." But Flodoard and the inscriptions give him a somewhat better character. See Hefele IV. 576, Gregorovius III. 269, and von Reumont II. 273.

(68; original 277): Gfrörer makes him the paramour of the younger Theodora, which on chronological grounds is more probable; but Hefele, Gregorovius, von Peumont, and Greenwood link him with the elder Theodora. This seems to be the meaning of Liutprand (II. 47 and 48), who says that she fell in love with John for his great beauty, and actually forced him to sin (secumque hunc scortari non solum voluit, verum etiam atque etiam compulit). She could not stand the separation from her lover, and called him to Rome. Baronius treats John X. as a pseudopapa. Muratori, Duret, and Hefele dissent from Liutprand and give John a somewhat better character, without, however, denying his relation to Theodora. See Hefele, IV. 579 sq.

(69; original 278): Liutprand, Antapodosis, III. 43 (Migne, l.c., 852): "Papam [John X.]custodia maniciparunt, in qua non multo post ea defunctus; aiunt enim quod cervical super os eius imponerent, sicque cum pessime su ffocarent. Quo mortuo ipsius Marotiae filium Johannem nomine [John XI.] quem ex Sergio papa meretrix genuerat, papam constituunt." The parentage of John XI. from pope Sergius is adopted by Gregorovius, Dümmler, Greenwood, and Baxmann, but disputed by Muratori, Hefele, and Gfrörer, who maintain that John XI. was the son of Marozia’s husband, Alberic I., if they ever were married. For, according to Benedict of Soracte, Marozia accepted him "non quasi uxor, sed in consuetudinem malignam." Albericus Marchio was an adventurer before he became Markgrave, about 897, and must not be confounded with Albertus Marchio or Adalbert the Rich of Tuscany. See Gregorovius, III. 275; von Reumont, II. 228, 231, and the genealogical tables in Höfler, Vol. I., Append. V. and VI.

(70; original 279): See the account in Liutprand III. 44.

(71; original 280): Among the charges of the Synod against him were that he appeared constantly armed with sword, lance, helmet, and breastplate, that he neglected matins and vespers, that he never signed himself with the sign of the cross, that he was fond of hunting, that he had made a boy of ten years a bishop, and ordained a bishop or deacon in a stable, that he had mutilated a priest, that he had set houses on fire, like Nero, that he had committed homicide and adultery, had violated virgins and widows high and low, lived with his father’s mistress, converted the pontifical palace into a brothel, drank to the health of the devil, and invoked at the gambling-table the help of Jupiter and Venus and other heathen demons! The emperor Otho would not believe these enormities until they, were proven, but the bishops replied, that they were matters of public notoriety requiring no proof. Before the Synod convened John XII. had made his escape from Rome, carrying with him the portable part of the treasury of St. Peter. But after the departure of the emperor he was readmitted to the city, restored for a short time, and killed in an act of adultery ("dum se cum viri cujusdam uxore oblectaret") by the enraged husband of his paramour. or by, the devil ("a diabolo est percussus"). Liutprand, De rebus gestis Ottonis (in Migne, Tom. XXXVI. 898-910). Hefele (IV. 619) thinks that he died of apoplexy.

(72; original 281): A full account of this Synod see in Liutprand, De rebus gestis Ottonis, and in Baronius, Annal. ad ann 963. Comp. also Greenwood, Bk VIII. ch. 12, Gfrörer, vol. III., p. iii., 1249 sqq., Giesebrecht, I. 465 and 828, and Hefele, IV. 612 sqq. Gfrörer, without defending John XII., charges Otho with having first violated the engagement (p. 1253). The pope was three times summoned before the Synod, but the answer came from Tivoli that he had gone hunting. Baronius, Floss, and Hefele regard this synod as uncanonical.

(73; original 282): Baronius, ad ann. 964, pronounced the document spurious, chiefly because it is very inconvenient to his ultramontane doctrine. It is printed in Mon. Germ. iv.2 (Leges, II. 167), and in a more extensive form from a MS. at Treves in Leonis VIII. privilegium de investituris, by H. J. Floss, Freib., 1858. This publication has changed the state of the controversy in favor of a genuine element in the document. See the discussion in Hefele, IV. 622 sqq.

(74; original 283): This antipathy found its last expression and termination in the expulsion of the Austrians from Lombardy and Venice, and the formation of a united kingdom of Italy.

(75; original 284): Ditmar of Merseburg, the historian of Henry II., expresses the sentiment of that time when he says (Chron. IV. 22): "Neither the climate nor the people suit our countrymen. Both in Rome and Lombardy treason is always at work. Strangers who visit Italy expect no hospitality: everything they require must be instantly paid for; and even then they must submit to be over-reached and cheated, and not unfrequently to be poisoned after all."

(76; original 285): "Quid hunc, rev. Patres, in sublimi solio residentem veste purpurea et aurea radiantem, quid hunc, inqam, esse censetis? Nimirum si caritate destituitur, solaque inflatur et extollitur, Antichristus est, in templo Dei sedens, et se ostendens tamquam sit D Eus. Si autem nec caritate fundatur, nec scientia erigitur, in templo Dei tamquam statua, tanquam idolum est, a quo responsa petere, marmora consulere est."

(77; original 286): The acts of this Synod were first published in the Magdeburg Centuries, then by Mansi, Conc. XIX. 107, and Pertz, Mon. V. 658. Baronius pronounced them spurious, and interspersed them with indignant notes; but Mansi (p. 107) says: "Censent vulgo omnes, Gerbertum reipsa et sincere recitasse acta concilii vere habiti." See Gieseler, Greenwood (Book VIII. ch. 6), and Hefele (IV. 637 sqq.). Hefele pronounces the speech schismatical.

(78; original 287): He is called Crescentius de Theodora, and seems to have died in a convent about 984. Some make him the son of Pope John X. and the elder Theodora, others, of the younger Theodora. See Gregorovius, III. 407 sqq; von Reumont, II. 292 sqq.; and the genealogy of the Crescentii in Höfler, I. 300.

(79; original 288): Gerbert (afterwards pope Sylvester II.) called this Bonifacius a "Malefactor," (Malifacius) and "horrendum monstrum, cunctos mortales nequitia superans, etiam prioris pontificis sanguine cruentus."Gregorovius, III. 410.

(80; original 289): The Tusculan family claimed descent from Julius Caesar and Octavian. See Gregorovius, IV. 10, and Giesebrecht II. 174; also the genealogical table of Höfler at the close of Vol. I.

(81; original 290): Baronius, however, says that Stephen VIII. (939-942) was a German, and for this reason opposed by the Romans. Bruno was only twenty-four years old when elected. Höfler (I. 94 sqq.) gives him a very high character.

(82; original 291): See preceding section, p. 290.

(83; original 292): According to several Italian writers he was poisoned by Stephania, under the disguise of a loving mistress, in revenge of the murder of Crescentius, her husband. Muratori and Milman accept the story, but it is not mentioned by Ditmar (Chron. IV. 30), and discredited by Leo, Gfrörer, and Greenwood. Otho had restored to the son of Stephania all his father’s property, and made him prefect of Rome. The same remorseless Stephania is said to have admininistered subtle poison to pope Sylvester II.

(84; original 293): See Gfrörer, III. P. III. 1550 sq. He regards Sylvester II. one of the greatest of popes and statesmen who developed all the germs of the system, and showed the way to his successors. Comp. on him Milman, Bk. V. ch. 13; Giesebrecht, I. 613 sqq. and 690 sqq.

(85; original 294): His historian, bishop Thitmar or Ditmar of Merseburg, relates that Henry never held carnal intercourse with his wife, and submitted to rigid penances and frequent flagellations for the subjugation of animal passions. But Hase (§ 160, tenth ed.) remarks: "Die Mönche, die er zu Gunsten der Bisthümer beraubt hat, dachten ihn nur eben von der Hölle gerettet; auch den Heiligenschein der jungfraeulichen Kaiserinhat der Teufel zu verdunkeln gewusst." Comp. C. Schurzfleisch, De innocentia Cunig., Wit., 1700. A. Noel, Leben der heil. Kunigunde, Luxemb. 1856. For a high and just estimate of Henry’s character see Giesebrecht II. 94-96. "The legend," he says, "describes Henry as a monk in purple, as a penitent with a crown, who can scarcely drag along his lame body; it places Kunigunde at his side not as wife but as a nun, who in prayer and mortification of the flesh seeks with him the path to heaven. History gives a very different picture of king Henry and his wife. It bears witness that he was one of the most active and energetic rulers that ever sat on the German throne, and possessed a sharp understanding and a power of organization very rare in those times. It was a misfortune for Germany that such a statesman had to spend most of his life in internal and external wars. Honorable as he was in arms, he would have acquired a higher fame in times of peace."

(86; original 295): Rodulfus Glaber, Histor. sui temporis, IV. 5 (in Migne, Tom. 142, p. 979): "puer ferme (fere) decennis;" but in V. 5: "fuerat sedi ordinatus quidam puer circiter annorum duodecim, contra jus nefasque." Hefele stated, in the first ed. (IV. 673), that Benedict was eighteen when elected. In the second ed. (p. 706) he corrects himself and makes him twelve years at his election.

(87; original 296): Isa. 3:1-4.

(88; original 297): Gregorovius, IV. 42, says: "Mit Benedict IX. erreichte das Papstthum aussersten Grad des sittlichen Verfalls, welcher nach den Gesetzen der menschlichen Natur den Umschlag zum Bessern erzeugt."

(89; original 298): Bonitho, ed. Jaffé p. 50: "Post multa turpia adulteria et homicidia manibus Buis perpetrata, postremo cum vellet consobrinam accipere coniugem, filiam scilicet Girardi de Saxo, et ille diceret: nullo modo se daturum nisi renunciaret pontificatui ad quendam sacerdotem Johannem se contulit." A similar report is found in the Annales Altahenses. But Steindorff and Hefele ([V. 707) discredit the marriage project as a malignant invention or fable.

(90; original 299): An old catalogue of popes (in Muratori, Script. III. 2, p. 345) states the sum as mille librae denariorum Papensium, but Benno as librae mille quingentae. Others give two thousand pounds as the sum. Otto of Freising adds that Benedict reserved besides the Peter’s pence from England. See Giesebrecht, II. 643, and Hefele IV. 707.

(91; original 300): Migne, Tom. 141, p. 1343. Steindorff and Hefele (IV. 708) dissent from this usual view of a three-fold schism, and consider Gregory, as the only pope. But all three were summoned to the Synod of Sutri and deposed; consequently they must all have claimed possession.

(92; original 301): The sources differ in the distribution of the work between the two synods: some assign it to Sutri, others to Rome, others divide it. Steindorff and Hefele (IV. 710) assume that Gregory and Sylvester were deposed at Sutri; Benedict (who did not appear at Sutri) was deposed in Rome. All agree that the new pope was elected in Rome.

(93; original 302): See Jaffé, Steindorff, and Hefele (IV. 711 sq.).

(94; original 303): According to the Annal. Corb., Suidger was elected "canonice as synodice … unanimi cleri et populi electione."

(95; original 304): So says Wibert, his friend and biographer, but Bonitho reports that Hildebrand induced him to submit first to a Roman election, since a pope elected by the emperor was not an apostolicus, but an apostaticus. See Baxmann, II. 215-217. Comp. also Hunkler: Leo IX. und seine Zeit. Mainz, 1851

  1. 此表根据雅费 (Jaffé)（《教皇文书录》，Regesta）、波特哈斯特 (Potthast)（《中世纪史书目》，Bibl. Hist. Medii AEvi，补编，259—267页）及其他资料编成。从使徒彼得到利奥十三世，教皇总数为263位。

    标有星号的皇帝均由教皇加冕，其余只是德意志的国王和皇帝。↩︎

  2. 革利免五世 (Clement V.) 于1309年将教皇驻地迁至阿维尼翁 (Avignon)，他的继任者继续在那里居住七十年，直至贵格利十一世。此后，罗马教皇与阿维尼翁教皇之间发生了长达四十年的分裂。↩︎

  3. 腓特烈三世 (Frederick III.) 是最后一位在罗马加冕的皇帝。他以后的所有继任者，除查理七世 (Charles VII.) 和弗朗西斯一世 (Francis I.) 外，都出自哈布斯堡家族 (Hapsburg)。↩︎

  4. 《十六、十七世纪的罗马教皇》(Die Römischen Paepste des 16 und 17 ten Jahrhunderts)，第I部分，第44页（第2版）。↩︎

  5. 教会使节（Apocrisiarius，ἀποκρισιάριος*，或 ἄγγελος*），responsalis。杜康日 (Du Cange) 将其定义为：“Nuntius, Legatus … praesertim qui a pontifice Romano, vel etiam ab archiepiscopis ad comitatum mittebantur, quo res ecclesiarum suarum peragerent, et de iis ad principem referrent.”（使者、使节……尤其指罗马教皇或大主教派往宫廷，办理各自教会事务并就此向君主陈报的人。）罗马派往君士坦丁堡的代表通常选自助祭。按照杜康日 Apocrisiarius 词条的记载，贵格利是第五位在君士坦丁堡担任这一职务的罗马助祭。↩︎

  6. 参见上文§ 10。↩︎

  7. 贵格利在致约翰的信（第V卷，18，见米涅版贵格利《著作集》〔Opera〕，第III卷，740）以及致皇帝毛里基乌斯 (Mauricius) 的信（第V卷，20，见米涅第III卷，747）中提及此事，但在两处都说，教皇从未要求或使用“hoc temerarium nomen”（这一僭妄的称号）。……“Certe pro beati Petri apostolorum principis honore, per venerandam Chalcedonensem synodum Romano pontifici oblatum est [nomen istud blasphemiae]. Sed nullus eorum unquam hoc singularitatis nomine uti consensit, dum privatum aliquid daretur uni, honore debito sacerdotes privarentur universi. Quid est ergo quod nos huius vocabuli gloriam et oblatam non quaerimus, et alter sibi hanc arripere at non oblatam praesumit?”（诚然，为尊荣使徒之首、有福的彼得，可敬的迦克墩会议曾将〔这个亵渎的称号〕奉给罗马教皇。但他们没有一人愿意使用这个独尊的称号，因为若将某种特权授予一人，就会剥夺所有祭司应有的尊荣。那么，我们连别人奉上的这一称号的荣耀都不追求，另一个人却胆敢攫取无人奉给他的称号，这究竟是怎么回事？）然而，严格说来，贵格利所认定的事实并不完全正确。利奥只是在迦克墩会议第三次会议上，一份控告狄奥斯库鲁斯 (Dioscurus) 的文书中，被称作 οἰκουμενικὸς ἀρχιεπίσκοπος*。教皇代表署名为：Vicarii apostolici universalis ecclesiae Papae（普世教会之使徒教皇的代表），希腊人将其译为：τῆς οἰκουμενικῆς*ἐκκλησία*ς ἐπισκόπου*。教皇声称自己是普世教会的教皇（而非主教）。参见赫费勒 (Hefele)《会议史》(Conciliengesch.) II，526。据说，博尼法斯三世 (Boniface III.) 于606年从皇帝福卡斯 (Phocas) 获得一道敕令，称彼得的教座为“caput omnium ecclesiarum”（众教会之首），于是公开采用了 universalis episcopis（普世主教）这一称号。在公元682—685年的《教皇日用文书集》(Liber Diurnus) 中，它以自行采用的称号出现，七世纪以后则屡见不鲜。然而，教会法学家把“universalis ecclesiae episcopus”（普世教会的主教）与“episcopus universalis”或“oecumenicus”（普世主教）加以区别，认为后者意指对其他主教的教区拥有直接的管辖权，而从前并不承认教皇具有此权。但按照1870年的梵蒂冈制度，他是众主教的主教，居于每一位主教以及全体主教之上；所有主教都不过是他的代理人，正如他自己是基督的代理人一样。参见拙著《基督教世界的信经》(Creeds of Christendom)，I，151。↩︎

  8. 参见第V卷，18—21的书信（米涅 III，738—751）。他的前任伯拉纠二世 (Pelagius II.，578—590年) 已曾强烈谴责约翰采用这一称号，同时声明自己也不使用它，但仍明确主张彼得教座的普世首席权。参见米涅，第LXXII卷，739，以及巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius)，ad ann. 587（587年条）。↩︎

  9. 《书信》(Ep.) V，43：致优洛吉乌斯和阿纳斯塔修斯两位主教（ad Eulogium et Anastasium episcopos）；VI，60；VII，34、40。↩︎

  10. 《书信》(Ep.) VII，13：“Ego autem confidenter dico quia quisquis se universalem sacerdotem vocat, vel vocari desiderat, in elatione sua Antichristum praecurrit, quia superbiendo se caeteris praeponit.”（但我确信地说，凡自称或希望被称为普世祭司的人，都在其狂傲中作了敌基督的先锋，因为他骄傲自大，将自己置于其余众人之上。）↩︎

  11. “Servus servorum Dei”（上帝众仆之仆）。见执事约翰 (Joa. Diaconus)《贵格利传》(Vit. Greg.) II. 1，以及《教皇日用文书集》(Lib. Diurnus)，载米涅版，第CV卷，23。奥古斯丁早先已曾署名为：“Servus Christi, et per ipsum servus servorum ejus”（基督的仆人，并借着他作他众仆人的仆人；《书信》217，ad Vitalem〔致维塔利斯〕）。参照《马太福音》二十26；二十三11。富尔根提乌斯 (Fulgentius) 自称“Servorum Christi famulus”（基督众仆人的仆役）。教皇们在圣周于圣彼得大堂炫耀性地为乞丐洗脚，效法基督的榜样，却指望国王和王后亲吻他们的脚趾。↩︎

  12. 他“ad Phocam imperatorem”（致皇帝福卡斯）的信，见《书信》(Ep.) XIII. 31（米涅版III. 1281），开头说：“Gloria in excelsis Deo, qui juxta quod scriptum est, immutat tempora et transfert regna”（荣耀归于至高处的上帝；正如经上所记，他改变时候，转移国权）。参照他“ad Leontiam imperatricen”（致皇后莱昂蒂娅）的信（《书信》XIII. 39）。↩︎

  13. 吉本 (Gibbon，第46章) 说：“作为臣民和基督徒，贵格利的本分是顺服既立的政府；但他以欢欣的喝彩迎接那刺客的得势，却给这位圣徒的品格留下了不可磨灭的耻辱污点。”米尔曼 (Milman，II. 83) 说：“贵格利名声上最黑暗的污点，是他对皇帝毛里基乌斯败亡所表现的残酷而不合基督徒身份的得意，以及他对福卡斯卑躬屈膝、阿谀奉承的赞美；后者是历来攫取君士坦丁堡皇位者中最可憎、最嗜血的暴君。”蒙塔朗贝尔 (Montalembert，II. 116) 说：“这是贵格利一生中唯一的污点。我们既不试图掩饰，也不试图开脱……即使在最伟大、最圣洁的凡人身上，德行也像智慧一样，总在某一方面有所不足。”随同巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius) 及其他罗马天主教史家作如下推想，乃是宽厚之举：贵格利虽然通常消息十分灵通，但当他对福卡斯登位表示过分的喜悦时，只知道登位这件事，而不知道其登位所用的血腥手段。对于他写给法国那位放荡邪恶、如复仇女神般的布伦希尔德 (Brunhilde) 的谄媚书信，也必须假定他同样不知实情。否则，我们在这里所见的，便是后来贵格利十三世欢呼胡格诺派信徒遭到可怕屠杀时那种恶毒快意的小规模先声。↩︎

  14. 原文如下：“Hic [Phocas] rogante papa Bonifacio statuit Romanae et apostolicae ecclesia caput esse omniuim ecclesiarum, quia ecclesia Constantinopolitana primam se omnium rum scribebat.”（此人〔福卡斯〕应教皇博尼法斯之请，规定罗马使徒教会为众教会之首，因为君士坦丁堡教会自称居于众教会之首。）执事保罗 (Paulus Diaconus)，《伦巴第人事迹》(De Gest. Lomb.) IV，第7章，载穆拉托里 (Muratori)《意大利史料》(Rer. Ital.) I. 465。然而，这一后来屡被抄录的记载，其真实性是可疑的。争论双方都曾滥用它。它既不是罗马首席权的首次宣告，也不是排他性首席权的宣告，更不是废除君士坦丁堡主教的“普世牧首”称号。参照格林伍德 (Greenwood)，第II卷，239及下页。↩︎

  15. 《书信》(Ep.) VII. 40（米涅版III. 899）。将彼得的三大教座相提并论——在本质统一中的教阶三个位格——似乎完全是贵格利的独创，后来再没有罗马教皇使用过。这对于罗马彼得教座的独一首席权是致命的，而这一首席权正是教皇主义的本质。↩︎

  16. 《书信》(Ep.) VIII. 30（III. 933）。↩︎

  17. 《书信》(Epist.) V. 20（III. 745）。他引用教皇主义最爱用的经文为证：《约翰福音》二十一17；《路加福音》二十二31；《马太福音》十六18。↩︎

  18. 例如 Universalis Episcopus（普世主教，博尼法斯三世在贵格利死后一年使用）、Pontifex Maximus, Summus Pontifex, Virarius Christi（最高祭司、至高教皇、基督的代理人），甚至“ipsius Dei in terris Virarius”（上帝自己在地上的代理人；Conc. Trid. VI. De reform., c. 1〔特伦托会议，第VI次会议，《改革法令》，第1章〕）。先是彼得的代理人，继而是基督的代理人，最后竟是全能上帝的代理人！↩︎

  19. 《呈献书信》(Ep. missoria)，第3章（米涅版I. 513）：“Primum quidem fundamenta historice ponimus; deinde per significatinem typicam in artem fidei fabricam mentis erigimus; ad extremum per moralitatus gratiam, quasi superducto aedificium colore vestimus.”（我们首先奠定历史的根基；然后借着预表的意义，将心灵的建筑竖立为信仰的堡垒；最后借着道德教诲的恩益，仿佛涂上色彩，装饰这座建筑。）↩︎

  20. 见《古今圣诗》(Hymns Ancient and Modem)。↩︎

  21. · 参照巴姆比 (Barmby)《大贵格利》(Greg. the Gr.)，188—190页；劳 (Lau)，262页；埃伯特 (Ebert)，I. 519。↩︎

  22. 或作卢伊特普兰德 (Luitprand)，约生于690年，卒于744年。另有一位同名的伦巴第史家，曾任帕维亚 (Pavia) 主教座堂执事，后任克雷莫纳 (Cremona) 主教，卒于972年。↩︎

  23. 吉本实际上把这些称号归于查理·马特 (Charles Martel)；布赖斯 (Bryce，40页) 则认为，这些称号最初是授予丕平 (Pepin) 的。贵格利二世早在724年便已称查理·马特为“Patricius”（贵族长；见米涅版《查理大帝著作集》〔Opera Caroli M.〕II. 69）。贵格利三世于739年送给他 ipsas sacratissimas claves confessionis beati Petri quas vobus ad regnum dimisimus（我们为使你掌权而送给你的、有福彼得墓所的至圣钥匙；同上，66页），这意味着将罗马的世俗统治权转交给他。↩︎

  24. 米尔曼（第IV书，第9章）说，但丁 (Dante) 忠实记录了民间天主教传统，采纳了那则将查理置于“地狱最底层深坑”的定罪传说。然而，我在但丁作品中找不到提及他的地方。《天堂篇》(Parad.) 第VIII、IX歌中的查理·马特完全是另一个人，乃是匈牙利国王，卒于1301年。见维特 (Witte)《但丁》(Dante)，667页，以及凯里 (Carey) 对《天堂篇》VIII. 53的注释。关于查理·马特与博尼法斯的关系，见雷特贝格 (Rettberg)《德国教会史》(Kirchengesch. Deutschlands)，I. 306及下页。↩︎

  25. 或拼作 Pipin、Pippin、Pippinus。最后一种拼法见于他自己的文书。↩︎

  26. 然而，雷特贝格（I. 385及下页）认为博尼法斯全然没有参与丕平的登位和加冕，反而将他描述为颇不赞同此事。他的论据是某些编年史家对此缄默不言，以及如下的不大可能之处：倘若教皇的使节已先行施行祝圣礼，教皇便不会再重复施行。↩︎

  27. 这是巴罗尼乌斯在 ad ann. 755（755年条）中的列举。其他人对其范围有不同的界定。参照维尔奇 (Wiltsch)《教会地理与统计》(Kirchl. Geographie und Statistik)，I，246及下页。↩︎

  28. 君士坦丁将拉特兰宫 (Lateran palace) 的一部分赐给教皇作为住所，并赋予教会持有不动产及接受个人遗赠地产的权利。这便是《君士坦丁赠礼》(Donatio Constantini) 那则虚构故事的薄弱根据。↩︎

  29. 《地狱篇》(Inferno) xix. 115—118：

    “Ahi Costantin, di quanto mal fu matre,

    Non la tua conversion, ma quella dote,

    Che da te presse il primo ricco patre!”

    “唉，君士坦丁，多少祸患由此而生，

    不是由于你的归信，而是由于那份赠礼，

    那第一位富有的教父从你手中所得的赠礼！”↩︎

  30. 《天堂篇》(Paradiso) XX. 57—60；VI. 94—97。朗费罗 (Longfellow) 译本。↩︎

  31. 约瑟夫·德·迈斯特 (Joseph de Maistre)：“Cet homme est si grand que, la grandeur a pénétré son nom.”（此人如此伟大，以至伟大已渗入他的名字。）（第4章），↩︎

  32. 艾因哈德 (Eginhard) 说：“对于查理的出生、婴儿时期，甚至童年，写下任何一个字都是愚妄的，因为关于这个题目从来没有任何记载，也没有一个活着的人能够提供有关消息。”通常认为他于742年4月2日生于亚琛 (Aix-la-Chapelle)；但传说却把他说成私情所生的孩子，作为磨坊主的儿子在巴伐利亚 (Bavaria) 无拘无束地长大。在他掌握政权以前，他的名字只被提及两次：一次是在他父亲为教皇司提反二世 (Stephen II.) 举行的宫廷接待中，另一次是在阿基坦 (Aquitaine) 战役中作为见证人。↩︎

  33. 据艾因哈德（第33章）的列举；不过，他只列了21处，遗漏了纳博讷 (Narbonne)。查理将其财宝和动产的三分之一遗赠给各都主教教座。↩︎

  34. 阿尔布雷希特·丢勒 (Albrecht Dürer) 所绘那幅壮丽的查理肖像是一幅想象画，并无最早的画像为据。韦托 (Vétault) 刊载了几幅肖像，并加以讨论，见540页。↩︎

  35. 一月称 Wintermonat，二月称 Hornung，三月称 Lenz，四月称 Ostermonat，等等。见艾因哈德，第29章。↩︎

  36. 艾因哈德，第27章。↩︎

  37. 博叙埃 (Bossuet) 为他所有的征服辩护，因为这些征服扩展了基督教。他说：“Les conquêtes prodigieuses”，“furent la dilatation du règne de Dieu, et il se moutra très chrétien dans toutes ses aeuvres.”（这些惊人的征服扩展了上帝的国度，而他在一切作为中都表现得极为虔诚，堪称基督徒。）↩︎

  38. 教皇司提反三世确曾以极其激烈的言辞，反对查理第二次结婚，迎娶伦巴第国王之女德西德拉塔 (Desiderata)；但他所据的理由，不是查理与第一任妻子离婚——这本可成为一个很好的理由——而是反对与“伦巴第人那背信弃义、长满麻风、恶臭的种族，那几乎算不得人类的种族”联姻。查理娶了这位公主，使母亲甚为欢喜；但次年便休弃她，将她送回父亲那里。见米尔曼，第IV书，第12章（II. 439）。↩︎

  39. 艾因哈德说：“Maximo totius populi luctu”（举国人民极其悲痛）。↩︎

  40. 这次战败的历史根据见于艾因哈德，第9章。后来人们又为它加上了奇异的润饰，罗兰 (Roland) 也成为吟游诗人和诗人喜爱的题材，如泰鲁尔德 (Théroulde) 的《罗兰之歌》(Chanson de Roland)、蒂尔潘 (Turpin) 的《编年史》(Chroniqué)、博亚尔多 (Bojardo) 的《热恋的奥兰多》(Orlando Innamorato)、阿里奥斯托 (Ariosto) 的《疯狂的奥兰多》(Orlando Furioso)，等等。他那有魔力的号角响声如此洪亮，飞鸟闻声坠地而死，撒拉森全军也惊恐退却。他在比利牛斯山 (Pyrenees) 遭到攻击时，最后一次吹响号角，用力之猛，竟使颈上的血管迸裂；查理大帝在数英里外的圣让皮耶德波尔 (St. Jean Pied de Port) 听见了号声，却已来不及拯救

    “那些虽死犹生的亡者，

    在著名的龙塞瓦尔 (Roncevall) 阵亡的人们。”↩︎

  41. 《洛尔施编年史》(Annales Laurissenses)，ad ann. 801（801年条）：“Ipsa die sacratissima natalis Domini cum Rex ad Missam ante confessionem b. Petri Apostoli ab oratione surgeret, Leo P. coronam capriti ejus imposuit, et a cuncto Romanorum populo acclamatum est:, Karolo Augusto, a Deo coronato, magno et pacifico Imperatori Romanorum, vita et victoria!’ Et post Laudes ab Apostolico more antiquorum principum adoratus est, atque, ablato Patricii nomine, Imperator et Augustus est appellatus.”（就在主降生的至圣之日，国王参加弥撒，在有福的使徒彼得墓前祷告完毕起身时，教皇利奥将冠冕戴在他头上，全体罗马人民欢呼：“愿上帝所加冕的查理·奥古斯都，伟大而带来和平的罗马人皇帝，长享生命与胜利！”颂赞之后，教皇按照古代君主的礼仪向他俯拜；贵族长的称号被撤去，他被称为皇帝和奥古斯都。）参照艾因哈德《编年史》(Annal.)，ad ann. 800（800年条），以及《查理传》(Vita Car.)，第28章。↩︎

  43. 上引韦托及施塔克 (Stacke) 的著作均转载了这幅画。↩︎

  44. 米尔曼（II. 497）说：“法兰克福会议最充分地显示出查理大帝对教阶集团以及王国贵族所行使的权力，也显示出他的立法兼具多种性质、包罗万象的特点。法兰克福的集会既是王国的帝国议会或国会，也是教会会议。它交替审议纯属教会的事务和同样明确属于世俗的事务。查理大帝出席并主持了法兰克福会议。教规及其他法令主要以他的名义颁布。”↩︎

  45. Sanctae Ecclesiae tam pium ac devotum in servitio Dei rectorem.（圣教会在事奉上帝上如此虔诚而忠贞的治理者。）此外，用他自己的话说，就是 Devotus Ecclesiae defensor atque adjutor in omnibus apostolicae sedis（教会忠贞的捍卫者，并在一切事上作使徒教座的助手）。雷特贝格，I. 425，439及下页。↩︎

  46. 《艾因哈德编年史》(Ann. Einhardi)，ad. ann. 813（813年条；载米涅《教父集》〔Patrol.〕第104卷，478页）：Evocatum ad se apud Aquasgrani filium suum Illudovicum Aquitaniae regem, coronam illi imposuit et imperialis nominis sibi consortem fecit.（他将自己的儿子、阿基坦国王路易召至亚琛身边，给他戴上冠冕，使他与自己共享皇帝的称号。）司提反四世于816年拜访路易时，只是为他施行了属灵的祝圣礼。同样，路易也立自己的儿子洛泰尔 (Lothair) 为皇帝，后来他才于罗马由教皇加冕（823年）。↩︎

  47. 布赖斯，396页（第8版）。↩︎

  48. 弗里德里希·吕克特 (Friedrich Rückert) 将这则意味深长的德国传说写成诗，开篇如下：

    老巴巴罗萨，

    那位弗里德里希皇帝，

    在地下的宫殿中，

    受着魔法的禁锢。

    他从未死去，

    至今仍在那里活着；

    他藏身宫殿之中，

    坐下沉沉睡去。

    他已将帝国的荣光，

    一同带到地下，

    终有一天，时机来到，

    他将携着荣光归来，等等。↩︎

  49. 在所有皇帝中，唯有他被但丁置于地狱（《地狱篇》〔Inferno〕，x. 119）：

    “这里面便有弗里德里希二世。”↩︎

  50. 席勒 (Schiller) 称之为“die kaiserlose, die schreckliche Zeit”（没有皇帝的、可怕的时代）。↩︎

  51. 教皇庇护六世 (Pius VI.) 甚至亲自前往维也纳 (Vienna)，但当他伸手让大臣考尼茨 (Kaunitz) 亲吻时，大臣却握住他的手，同他握手。约瑟夫 (Joseph) 随后回访罗马，民众高呼迎接他：“Evviva il nostro imperatore!”（我们的皇帝万岁！）↩︎

  52. 但丁（《炼狱篇》〔Purgat.〕VII. 94）描写哈布斯堡的鲁道夫 (Rudolf of Hapsburg) 在炼狱中阴郁地独坐一旁，为自己疏忽了以下责任的罪而悲伤：

    “医治那使意大利丧命的创伤。”

    但丁厌倦了各城本土暴君与党派间无休止的争斗，渴望有某种统摄的权力，为他所挚爱却不幸的意大利恢复统一与和平。他在《论世界帝国》(De Monarchia) 一书中阐述了自己的政治思想。↩︎

  53. 他在给费施 (Fesch) 的另一封信中（《拿破仑一世通信集》〔Correspond. de l’ empereur Napol. Ier〕，第xi卷，第528页）写道：“Pour le pape je suis Charemagne. parce que comme Charlemagne je réunis la couronne de Prance à celle du Lombards et que mon empire confine avec l’ Orient.”（对教皇而言，我就是查理大帝，因为我像查理大帝一样，将法兰西的王冠与伦巴第的王冠合而为一，而且我的帝国与东方接壤。）转引自布赖斯 (Bryce)。↩︎

  54. 最早的见证见于阿纳斯塔修斯 (Anastasius) 几乎与事件同时代的《教皇列传》(Liber Pontificalis)，但最佳抄本中没有这段文字，故必是后来的插文。德林格 (Döllinger) 指出，这个传说虽可能较早已在民间口耳相传，却直到十三世纪中叶才确切地见诸文字。↩︎

  55. 序言开头说：“Isidorus Mercator servus Christi lectori conservo suo et parenti suo in Domino fideli (al. fidei) salutem.”（基督的仆人伊西多尔·墨尔卡托，向同作仆人、在主里忠信的父辈读者问安；另有异读 fidei。）“Mercator”这一别号见于30部最古老的抄本，迄今尚无解释。有人将它与马里乌斯·墨尔卡托 (Marius Mercator) 联系起来；他是一位居于君士坦丁堡的博学西方平信徒，曾撰文反对伯拉纠 (Pelagius)，并翻译过伪伊西多尔 (pseudo-Isidorus) 所利用的教会文献。另有人认为它是“Peccator”（罪人）的误写；这个称号是司铎和主教常用的自谦之称，例如圣帕特里克 (St. Patrick) 在其《忏悔录》中便如此自称，而这一写法见于3部抄本。另有数部抄本作“Mercatus”，相当于 redemptus（蒙救赎者），即“伊西多尔，基督所救赎的仆人”。参见欣希乌斯 (Hinschius) 和里希特 (Richter)，前引书。↩︎

  56. 原名为 decretale constitutum（教令规定）或 decretalis epistola（教令书信），后来称为 decretalis（教令）。参见里希特 (Richter)，前引书，第80页。↩︎

  57. 十五世纪的库萨的尼古拉 (Nicolaus de Cusa) 最先提出这是伪作，加尔文 (Calvin) 也曾指出这一点（《基督教要义》〔Inst.〕IV. 7, 11, 20）；随后《马格德堡世纪史》(Magdeburg Centuries) 证明了这一点，而加尔文派神学家大卫·布隆代尔 (David Blondel) 于1628年针对耶稣会士托雷斯 (Torres，拉丁名 Turrianus，1572年) 的辩护尝试，作出了更具决定性的证明。巴莱里尼兄弟 (Ballerini)、巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius)、贝拉明 (Bellarmin)、泰纳 (Theiner)、瓦尔特 (Walter)、默勒 (Möhler)、赫费勒 (Hefele) 以及其他罗马天主教学者承认这是伪作，但通常试图减轻其严重性，低估伪伊西多尔 (Pseudo-Isidor) 的独创性和影响。有些新教神学家则犯了相反的错误（里希特在前引书第117页中公允地指出了这一点）。↩︎

  58. “Dominis meis beatissimis Petro et Paulo, et per eos etiam beato Sylvestro Patri nostro summo pontifici, et universalis urbis Romae papae, et omnibus ejus successoribus pontificibus . . concedimus palatium imperii nostri Lateranense ... deinde diadema, videlicet coronam capitis nostri simulque pallium, vel mitram .... . et omnia imperialia indumenta ... et imperialia sceptra . . et omnem possessionem imperialis culminis et gloriam potestatis nostrae ... Unde ut pontificalis apex non vilescat, sed magis amplius quam terreni imperii dignitas et gloriae potentia decoretur, ecce tam palatium nostrum, ut praedictum est, quamque Pomanae vobis et omnes Italiae seu occidentalium regionum provincias, loca et civitates beatissimo pontifici nostro, Sylvestro universali papae, concedimus atque relinquimus.”（我们将帝国的拉特兰宫殿……以及冠冕，即我们头上的王冠，连同披肩或礼冠……以及一切皇帝服饰……皇帝的权杖……帝位的一切产业和我们权势的荣耀，赐予我们至福的主彼得和保罗，并通过他们赐予有福的西尔维斯特——我们的父、最高教皇、全罗马城的教皇——以及继任他的所有教皇……因此，为使教皇的至尊地位不致卑微，反而获得比地上帝国的尊位及其荣耀的权势更大的尊荣，看哪，我们将上述宫殿，以及罗马城、意大利和西方各地区的一切行省、地方与城市，赐予并留给我们至福的教皇、普世教皇西尔维斯特。）载于米涅 (Migne)，第130卷，第249页及下页。↩︎

  59. 君士坦丁 (Constantine) 在其所谓受洗之际向西尔维斯特 (Sylvester) 作出赠予一事，最早见于《西尔维斯特行传》(Acta Sylvestri)，随后哈德良一世 (Hadrian I.) 在致查理大帝 (Charlemagne) 的一封信中（780年）也加以叙述。九世纪时，这份伪造的文书出现了。早在999年，皇帝奥托三世 (Otho III.) 就已察觉它是伪作；约于1440年，洛伦佐·瓦拉 (Laurentius Valla) 在《关于被误信并属捏造的君士坦丁赠予》(De falso credita et ementita Constantini donatione) 中证明了这一点。这份文书如今已被一致认定为虚构，尽管巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius) 曾为赠予一事本身辩护。↩︎

  60. 以下人物曾被提出为作者：美因茨 (Mayence) 的利未人（执事）本尼迪克特 (Benedictus Levita)，他约于847年编成的《敕令集》(Capitularium) 有几处与《教令集》逐字相同（布隆代尔〔Blondel〕、克努斯特〔Knust〕、瓦尔特〔Walter〕之说）；苏瓦松的罗塔德 (Rothad of Soissons)（菲利普斯〔Phillips〕、格弗勒〔Gfrörer〕之说）；美因茨大主教奥特加尔 (Otgar)，他在教士反对虔诚者路易 (Louis the Pious) 的叛乱中起过重要作用（巴莱里尼兄弟〔Ballerinii〕、瓦瑟施勒本〔Wasserschleben〕之说）；兰斯大主教埃博 (Ebo)，即欣克马尔 (Hincmar) 的前任和那次叛乱的领袖，或该教区内某位不知名的教士（魏茨泽克〔Weizsäcker〕、冯·诺尔登〔von Noorden〕、欣希乌斯〔Hinschius〕、里希特〔Richter〕、巴克斯曼〔Baxmann〕之说）。其中的重复之处，表明它可能出自多人之手，并逐渐增补而成。↩︎

  61. 《尼古拉一世于865年致高卢全体主教书》(Nicolai I. Epist. ad universos episcopos Galliae ann. 865)（曼西〔Mansi〕，第xv卷，第694页及下页）：“Decretales epistolae Rom. Pontificum sunt recipiendae, etiamsi non sunt canonum codici compaginatae: quoniam inter ipsos canones unum b. Leonis capitulum constat esse permixtum, quo omnia decretalia constituta sedes apostolicae custodiri mandantur.—Itaque nihil interest, utrum sint omnia decretalia sedis Apost. constituta inter canones conciliorum immixta, cum omnia in uno copore compaginare non possint et illa eis intersint, quae firmitatem his quae desunt et vigorem suum assignet.—Sanctus Gelasius (quoque) non dixit suscipiendas decretales epistolas quae inter canones habentur, nec tantum quas moderni pontifices ediderunt, sed quas beatissimi Papae diversis temporibus ab urbe Roma dederunt.”（罗马教皇的教令书信，即使未收入教规汇编，也应当接受；因为众所周知，这些教规中收有有福的利奥的一条规定，命人遵守使徒之座的一切教令规定。——因此，使徒之座的一切教令规定是否都编入会议教规之中，并无关系，因为不可能将它们全部编成一册，而已经收入的规定，足以赋予尚未收入者以效力和权威。——圣格拉修斯也并未说，应当接受的教令书信仅限于教规所载者，也不只是近代教皇所颁布者，而是至福的教皇们在不同时期从罗马城颁发的那些书信。）↩︎

  63. “Rotha dum canonice ... dejectum et a Nicolao papa non regulariter, sed potentialiter restitutum.”（罗塔德依教规……被罢免，而教皇尼古拉并非依规章、乃是凭权力使他复职。）参见巴克斯曼 (Baxmann)，II. 26。↩︎

  64. 据奥克森提乌斯 (Auxentius) 和武尔加里乌斯 (Vulgarius) 所述，侮辱福尔摩苏斯 (Formosus) 尸体一事的主使者是教皇斯蒂芬七世 (Stephen VII.)；柳特普兰德 (Liutprand) 则将此事归于898年的塞尔吉乌斯三世 (Sergius III.)，当时他是与约翰九世 (John IX.) 对立的教皇。巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius) 推测，柳特普兰德将 Stephanus 误写为 Sergius。赫费勒 (Hefele) 赞同此说，见《宗教会议史》(Conciliengesch.) IV. 561及后。↩︎

  66. 柳特普兰德 (Liutprand)，《报应录》(Antapodosis)，II. 48（佩尔茨〔Pertz〕，V. 297；米涅〔Migne〕，CXXXVI. 827）：Theodora, scortum impudens ... (quod dictu etiam foedissimum est), Romanae civitatis non inviriliter monarchiam obtinebat. Quae duas habuit natas, Marotiam atque Theodoram, sibi non solum coaequales, verum etiam Veneris exercitio promptiores. Harum Marotia ex Papa Sergio-Joannem, qui post Joannis Ravennatis obitum Romanae Ecclesiae obtinuit dignitatem, nefario genuit adulterio, 等。（狄奥多拉，这无耻的娼妇……〔这话即使说出来也极其污秽〕，以不让须眉的手腕独揽罗马城的大权。她有两个女儿，玛罗齐娅和狄奥多拉，不但与她不相上下，在情欲之事上更比她积极。其中玛罗齐娅与教皇塞尔吉乌斯邪淫，生下约翰；此人在拉文纳的约翰死后，取得了罗马教会的尊位。）同章又称老狄奥多拉为“meretrix satis impudentissima, Veneris calore succensa”（极其无耻、欲火中烧的娼妇）。

    这位狄奥多拉 (Theodora) 是罗马执政官兼元老狄奥菲拉克图斯 (Theophylactus) 的妻子；后者可能出身拜占庭，901年见于路易三世 (Louis III.) 在罗马的法官之列。她自称“Senatrix”（女元老）。她是托斯卡纳的阿达尔贝特 (Adalbert of Tuscany)，号称“富有者”（卒于926年），以及教皇约翰十世 (John X.，卒于928年) 的情妇。然而，欧根尼乌斯·武尔加里乌斯 (Eugenius Vulgarius) 竟称她为“sanctissima et venerabilis matrona”（至圣而可敬的贵妇）！（参见迪姆勒〔Dümmler〕，前引书，第146页；赫费勒〔Hefele〕，IV. 575。）她的女儿玛罗齐娅 (Marozia，或 Maruccia，即 Maria 的指小形式，相当于德语 Mariechen) 是三人中最大胆、最成功的一位。她是教皇塞尔吉乌斯三世 (Sergius III.) 和图斯库卢姆伯爵阿尔贝里克一世 (Alberic I., Count of Tusculum，卒于926年) 的情妇，并曾数次结婚。比较柳特普兰德，III. 43和44。她通过儿子阿尔贝里克二世 (Alberic II.) 和孙子教皇约翰十二世 (John XII.) 延续了自己的统治。这些意志坚强的妇女虽有种种才干和影响力，却十分无知；小狄奥多拉的女儿们既不能读，也不能写，945年签名时用的是一个 + 号。（格雷戈罗维乌斯〔Gregorovius〕，III. 282及下页。）十一世纪操纵教皇权并使之蒙羞的图斯库卢姆诸教皇和克雷森蒂家族 (Crescentii)，都是这一血统的后裔。

    这段可耻统治的主要事实，有可靠的同时代天主教史料为据（如柳特普兰德、弗洛多阿尔德〔Flodoard〕、维罗纳的拉瑟里乌斯〔Ratherius of Verona〕、索拉克特的本尼迪克特〔Benedict of Soracte〕、热尔贝〔Gerbert〕以及罗马、兰斯等地宗教会议的记录）；巴罗尼乌斯和其他罗马天主教史家，也怀着虔诚的愤慨坦率地承认这些事实，却又将其转化为教皇权出于上帝的论据，认为教皇权堕落得越深，其权势的恢复便越显奇妙。默勒 (Möhler)（《教会史》〔Kirchgesch.〕，加马〔Gama〕编，II. 183）称塞尔吉乌斯三世、约翰十世、约翰十一世和约翰十二世为“可怕的教皇”，并说：“唯有罪行才能使人取得教皇的尊位！”另一些人则以教皇权当时并不独立为由，免除其罪责。罗马天主教徒从这一卖淫时期所能汲取的最佳教训，应是谦卑与仁爱。对于自称比别人更为圣洁的夸言，这段历史是严厉的斥责。↩︎

  67. 巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius) 沿用柳特普兰德 (Liutprand) 的说法，称塞尔吉乌斯 (Sergius) 为“homo vitiorum omnium servus”（为一切恶习所奴役的人）。但弗洛多阿尔德 (Flodoard) 和碑铭对他的品行评价稍好。参见赫费勒 (Hefele)，IV. 576；格雷戈罗维乌斯 (Gregorovius)，III. 269；冯·罗伊蒙特 (von Reumont)，II. 273。↩︎

  68. 格弗勒 (Gfrörer) 认为他是小狄奥多拉 (Theodora) 的情夫，从年代上看，这种说法更为可信；但赫费勒 (Hefele)、格雷戈罗维乌斯 (Gregorovius)、冯·珀蒙特 (von Peumont) 和格林伍德 (Greenwood) 则将他与老狄奥多拉联系起来。这似乎也是柳特普兰德 (Liutprand)（II. 47和48）的意思；他说，她因约翰容貌俊美而爱上他，甚至强迫他犯罪（secumque hunc scortari non solum voluit, verum etiam atque etiam compulit，她不但想让他与自己淫乱，还一再强迫他如此行）。她无法忍受与情人分离，便召他到罗马。巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius) 将约翰十世 (John X.) 视为 pseudopapa（伪教皇）。穆拉托里 (Muratori)、迪雷 (Duret) 和赫费勒不同意柳特普兰德的判断，对约翰的品行评价稍好，却也不否认他与狄奥多拉的关系。参见赫费勒，IV. 579及下页。↩︎

  69. 柳特普兰德 (Liutprand)，《报应录》(Antapodosis)，III. 43（米涅〔Migne〕，前引书，第852页）：“Papam [John X.]custodia maniciparunt, in qua non multo post ea defunctus; aiunt enim quod cervical super os eius imponerent, sicque cum pessime su ffocarent. Quo mortuo ipsius Marotiae filium Johannem nomine [John XI.] quem ex Sergio papa meretrix genuerat, papam constituunt.”（他们将教皇〔约翰十世〕囚禁，不久他便死于狱中；据说他们把枕头压在他嘴上，就这样将他残酷地闷死。他死后，他们立玛罗齐娅的儿子、名叫约翰的〔约翰十一世〕为教皇；他是这个娼妇与教皇塞尔吉乌斯所生。）约翰十一世是教皇塞尔吉乌斯之子的说法，为格雷戈罗维乌斯 (Gregorovius)、迪姆勒 (Dümmler)、格林伍德 (Greenwood) 和巴克斯曼 (Baxmann) 所采纳，却受到穆拉托里 (Muratori)、赫费勒 (Hefele) 和格弗勒 (Gfrörer) 的质疑；后者主张约翰十一世是玛罗齐娅的丈夫阿尔贝里克一世 (Alberic I.) 的儿子——如果这两人确曾结婚的话。因为据索拉克特的本尼迪克特 (Benedict of Soracte) 所述，玛罗齐娅接受他，“non quasi uxor, sed in consuetudinem malignam”（并非与他结为夫妻，而是维持不正当的关系）。阿尔贝里库斯侯爵 (Albericus Marchio) 在约897年成为边区侯爵以前，是个冒险家，不可将他与阿尔贝图斯侯爵 (Albertus Marchio)，即托斯卡纳的富有者阿达尔贝特 (Adalbert the Rich of Tuscany) 混为一谈。参见格雷戈罗维乌斯，III. 275；冯·罗伊蒙特 (von Reumont)，II. 228、231；以及赫夫勒 (Höfler)，第I卷，附录V、VI中的家谱表。↩︎

  70. 参见柳特普兰德 (Liutprand)，III. 44的记载。↩︎

  71. 宗教会议对他的指控包括：他经常佩剑、持矛，戴盔披甲；他忽略晨祷和晚祷；他从不在自己身上画十字；他喜好狩猎；他曾立一个十岁的男孩为主教，并在马厩里按立主教或执事；他曾残害一名司铎的肢体；他像尼禄一样纵火焚烧房屋；他曾杀人、奸淫，凌辱贵贱各阶层的处女和寡妇；他与父亲的情妇同居；他把教皇宫殿变成妓院；他举杯祝魔鬼健康；他在赌桌上求助于朱庇特 (Jupiter)、维纳斯 (Venus) 和其他异教邪神！皇帝奥托 (Otho) 在这些滔天罪行得到证实以前，不肯相信；主教们却回答说，这些都是众所周知、无须证明的事实。会议召开以前，约翰十二世 (John XII.) 已逃离罗马，随身带走了圣彼得府库中可携走的财物。但皇帝离去以后，他又获准入城，一度复位，随后在奸淫时（“dum se cum viri cujusdam uxore oblectaret”，当他与某人的妻子寻欢作乐时），被情妇盛怒的丈夫杀死，或被魔鬼击杀（“a diabolo est percussus”）。柳特普兰德，《奥托事迹》(De rebus gestis Ottonis)（载于米涅〔Migne〕，第XXXVI卷，第898–910页）。赫费勒 (Hefele)（IV. 619）认为他死于中风。↩︎

  72. 关于这次宗教会议的完整记载，见柳特普兰德 (Liutprand)《奥托事迹》(De rebus gestis Ottonis)，以及巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius)《编年史》(Annal. ad ann) 963年条。另参格林伍德 (Greenwood)，第VIII卷，第12章；格弗勒 (Gfrörer)，第III卷，第iii部分，第1249页及后；吉泽布雷希特 (Giesebrecht)，I. 465、828；赫费勒 (Hefele)，IV. 612及后。格弗勒并不为约翰十二世 (John XII.) 辩护，却指责奥托 (Otho) 首先违背了约定（第1253页）。会议曾三次传召教皇前来，但从蒂沃利 (Tivoli) 传来的答复却是，他已外出打猎。巴罗尼乌斯、弗洛斯 (Floss) 和赫费勒认为这次会议不合教会法规。↩︎

  73. 巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius) 在《编年史》(ad ann.) 964年条中断定这份文件是伪造的，主要因为它对他的教皇至上主义 (ultramontane) 教义十分不利。文件刊于《日耳曼史料集》(Mon. Germ.)，iv.2（Leges，II. 167）；H. J. 弗洛斯 (H. J. Floss) 又依据特里尔 (Treves) 的一份手稿，将较详尽的文本刊于《利奥八世关于授职权的特许状》(Leonis VIII. privilegium de investituris)，Freib.，1858年。这一出版物改变了争论的局面，使文件中含有真实成分的看法更占优势。有关讨论见赫费勒 (Hefele)，IV. 622及后。↩︎

  74. 这种反感最后表现为将奥地利人逐出伦巴第 (Lombardy) 和威尼斯 (Venice)，以及建立统一的意大利王国，并至此告终。↩︎

  75. 亨利二世 (Henry II.) 的史家、梅泽堡的迪特马尔 (Ditmar of Merseburg) 表达了当时人的感受。他说（《编年史》〔Chron.〕，IV. 22）：“无论气候还是人民，都不适合我们的同胞。在罗马和伦巴第，背叛无时不在作祟。到意大利来的异乡人不指望得到款待：凡有所需，都必须立即付钱；即便如此，他们仍不得不忍受敲诈和欺骗，甚至到头来还常常遭到毒害。”↩︎

  76. “Quid hunc, rev. Patres, in sublimi solio residentem veste purpurea et aurea radiantem, quid hunc, inqam, esse censetis? Nimirum si caritate destituitur, solaque inflatur et extollitur, Antichristus est, in templo Dei sedens, et se ostendens tamquam sit D Eus. Si autem nec caritate fundatur, nec scientia erigitur, in templo Dei tamquam statua, tanquam idolum est, a quo responsa petere, marmora consulere est.”（“尊敬的教父们，这人坐在高高的宝座上，身披紫袍金衣，光彩耀目；我问，你们认为他是什么人呢？诚然，若他缺乏爱心，只知自高自大，他就是敌基督，坐在上帝的殿里，自称为上帝。倘若他既不以爱心为根基，也不因学识而长进，那么他在上帝殿中就如一尊雕像、一个偶像；向他求问，无异于求问大理石。”）↩︎

  77. 这次宗教会议的议事录最初刊于《马格德堡世纪史》(Magdeburg Centuries)，后来又由曼西 (Mansi) 刊于《会议集》(Conc.)，XIX. 107，以及佩尔茨 (Pertz) 刊于《史料集》(Mon.)，V. 658。巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius) 断定这些记录是伪造的，并在其中穿插了愤慨的批注；但曼西（第107页）说：“Censent vulgo omnes, Gerbertum reipsa et sincere recitasse acta concilii vere habiti.”（人们普遍认为，格尔伯特确实忠实地记述了一次真实举行的会议的议事录。）参见吉泽勒 (Gieseler)、格林伍德 (Greenwood)（第VIII卷，第6章）及赫费勒 (Hefele)（IV. 637及后）。赫费勒判定这篇演说具有分裂教会的性质。↩︎

  78. 他被称为克雷申提乌斯·德·狄奥多拉 (Crescentius de Theodora)，似乎于984年左右死于一所修道院。有人说他是教皇约翰十世 (John X.) 与老狄奥多拉 (Theodora) 的儿子，也有人说他是小狄奥多拉的儿子。参见格雷戈罗维乌斯 (Gregorovius)，III. 407及后；冯·罗伊蒙特 (von Reumont)，II. 292及后；以及赫夫勒 (Höfler)，I. 300中的克雷申提乌斯家族谱系。↩︎

  79. 格尔伯特 (Gerbert，后来的教皇西尔维斯特二世〔Sylvester II.〕) 称这位博尼法修斯 (Bonifacius) 为“作恶者”(Malifacius)，又称他为“horrendum monstrum, cunctos mortales nequitia superans, etiam prioris pontificis sanguine cruentus.”（一个可怕的怪物，邪恶超过一切世人，甚至沾染了前任教皇的鲜血。）格雷戈罗维乌斯 (Gregorovius)，III. 410。↩︎

  80. 图斯库卢姆家族 (Tusculan family) 自称是尤利乌斯·凯撒 (Julius Caesar) 和屋大维 (Octavian) 的后裔。参见格雷戈罗维乌斯 (Gregorovius)，IV. 10；吉泽布雷希特 (Giesebrecht)，II. 174；另见赫夫勒 (Höfler) 第I卷卷末的家谱表。↩︎

  81. 不过，巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius) 说，司提反八世 (Stephen VIII.，939—942年) 是个德意志人，并因此遭到罗马人的反对。布鲁诺 (Bruno) 当选时年仅二十四岁。赫夫勒 (Höfler)（I. 94及后）对他的品格评价极高。↩︎

  82. 见前一节，第290页。↩︎

  83. 据几位意大利作家说，他遭斯特法尼娅 (Stephania) 毒害；她装作钟情的情妇，为被杀的丈夫克雷申提乌斯 (Crescentius) 报仇。穆拉托里 (Muratori) 和米尔曼 (Milman) 接受这个故事，但迪特马尔 (Ditmar)（《编年史》〔Chron.〕，IV. 30）并未提及，利奥 (Leo)、格弗勒 (Gfrörer) 和格林伍德 (Greenwood) 也不相信。奥托 (Otho) 曾将斯特法尼娅之子父亲的全部财产归还给他，并任命他为罗马长官。据说，这位同样冷酷无情的斯特法尼娅还曾给教皇西尔维斯特二世 (Sylvester II.) 施用隐秘的毒药。↩︎

  84. 参见格弗勒 (Gfrörer)，III，第III部分，第1550页及下页。他认为西尔维斯特二世 (Sylvester II.) 是最伟大的教皇和政治家之一，发展了这一体制的一切萌芽，并为继任者指明了道路。关于他，另参米尔曼 (Milman)，第V卷，第13章；吉泽布雷希特 (Giesebrecht)，I. 613及后、690及后。↩︎

  85. 为他作史的梅泽堡主教蒂特马尔或迪特马尔 (Thitmar or Ditmar of Merseburg) 记述，亨利从未与妻子有过肉体交合，并为制伏肉欲而承受严厉的补赎和频繁的鞭笞。但哈泽 (Hase)（第十版，§ 160）评论道：“他为使主教区得益而剥夺了修士们的财产；这些修士认为，他不过是勉强得以免下地狱；就连贞洁皇后的圣洁光环，魔鬼也懂得如何使之黯淡。”另参 C. 舒尔茨弗莱施 (C. Schurzfleisch)，《论库尼贡德的清白》(De innocentia Cunig.)，Wit.，1700年；A. 诺埃尔 (A. Noel)，《圣库尼贡德传》(Leben der heil. Kunigunde)，Luxemb.，1856年。对于亨利品格的崇高而公允的评价，见吉泽布雷希特 (Giesebrecht)，II. 94—96。他说：“传说把亨利描绘成身披紫袍的修士、头戴王冠的忏悔者，几乎拖不动自己跛足的躯体；又将库尼贡德置于他身旁，不是作为妻子，而是作为修女，在祷告和克制肉体中与他一同寻求通往天堂的道路。历史所呈现的亨利国王及其妻子的形象，却迥然不同。历史证明，他是德意志王位上历来最活跃、最有魄力的统治者之一，具有敏锐的理解力和那个时代极为罕见的组织才能。这样一位政治家却不得不将一生的大部分时光耗费于内外战争，这是德意志的不幸。他虽已在战场上赢得荣耀，若处于和平时代，本可获得更高的声誉。”↩︎

  86. 鲁道夫斯·格拉伯 (Rodulfus Glaber)，《当代史》(Histor. sui temporis)，IV. 5（载于米涅〔Migne〕，第142卷，第979页）：“puer ferme (fere) decennis”（一个约十岁的男孩）；但在V. 5中却说：“fuerat sedi ordinatus quidam puer circiter annorum duodecim, contra jus nefasque”（一个约十二岁的男孩，违背一切法度，被按立登上教座）。赫费勒 (Hefele) 在第一版（IV. 673）中说，本笃当选时十八岁。在第二版（第706页）中，他更正了自己的说法，认定本笃当选时十二岁。↩︎

  87. 《以赛亚书》3:1—4。↩︎

  88. 格雷戈罗维乌斯 (Gregorovius)，IV. 42，说：“在本笃九世 (Benedict IX.) 时，教皇制的道德败坏达到了极点；按照人性的规律，这种极端败坏会促成向善的转变。”↩︎

  89. 博尼托 (Bonitho)，雅费 (Jaffé) 编，第50页：“Post multa turpia adulteria et homicidia manibus Buis perpetrata, postremo cum vellet consobrinam accipere coniugem, filiam scilicet Girardi de Saxo, et ille diceret: nullo modo se daturum nisi renunciaret pontificatui ad quendam sacerdotem Johannem se contulit.”（在犯下许多可耻的奸淫并亲手杀人之后，他终于想娶自己的表妹，即吉拉尔多·德·萨克索〔Girardi de Saxo〕的女儿为妻；而她父亲说，除非他放弃教皇职位，否则决不把女儿嫁给他，于是他去找一位名叫约翰的司铎。）《阿尔泰希编年史》(Annales Altahenses) 中也有类似的记载。但施泰因多夫 (Steindorff) 和赫费勒 (Hefele)（[V. 707）不相信这一婚姻计划，认为是恶意的捏造或虚构。↩︎

  90. 一份古老的教皇名录（载于穆拉托里〔Muratori〕，Script. III. 2，第345页）说，这笔款项是 mille librae denariorum Papensium（一千磅帕维亚银币），本诺 (Benno) 却说是 librae mille quingentae（一千五百磅）。另有人说是两千磅。弗赖辛的奥托 (Otto of Freising) 还补充说，本笃此外还保留了来自英格兰的圣彼得便士税。参见吉泽布雷希特 (Giesebrecht)，II. 643；赫费勒 (Hefele)，IV. 707。↩︎

  91. 米涅 (Migne)，第141卷，第1343页。施泰因多夫 (Steindorff) 和赫费勒 (Hefele)（IV. 708）不赞同这种三方分裂的通行看法，认为格列高利是唯一的教皇。但三人都被传召到苏特里宗教会议 (Synod of Sutri)，并遭罢黜；因此，他们必定都曾声称自己拥有教皇职位。↩︎

  92. 史料对于两次宗教会议各自处理了哪些事项，说法不一：有些归于苏特里 (Sutri)，有些归于罗马，有些则分归两地。施泰因多夫 (Steindorff) 和赫费勒 (Hefele)（IV. 710）认为，格列高利和西尔维斯特是在苏特里被罢黜的；本笃（他没有到苏特里出席会议）则在罗马被罢黜。各方都同意，新教皇是在罗马选出的。↩︎

  93. 参见雅费 (Jaffé)、施泰因多夫 (Steindorff) 和赫费勒 (Hefele)（IV. 711及下页）。↩︎

  94. 据《科尔韦编年史》(Annal. Corb.)，苏伊德格 (Suidger) 是“canonice as synodice … unanimi cleri et populi electione.”（依照教会法规并经会议……由教士和民众一致选举）而当选的。↩︎

  95. 他的朋友兼传记作者维贝尔特 (Wibert) 如此说；但博尼托 (Bonitho) 记载，是希尔德布兰德 (Hildebrand) 劝他先接受罗马人的选举，因为由皇帝选出的教皇不是 apostolicus（使徒的继承者），而是 apostaticus（背道者）。参见巴克斯曼 (Baxmann)，II. 215—217。另参洪克勒 (Hunkler)：《利奥九世及其时代》(Leo IX. und seine Zeit.)，美因茨 (Mainz)，1851年。↩︎
